The card selects the applet by itselfOKAfter a power-up, GET UID answered 90 00 with 10 bytes without any SELECT, so the applet holds the "default selected" privilege.
The card ID is usableOKCard ID A1 FB 6A 0B F3 80 24 71 00 F7 (10 bytes).
The card ID stays the sameOKThe same card ID came back from all 5 power-ups.
The card answers every tapOK5 power-ups in a row, each followed by GET UID.
An app can select the applet by its AIDOKSELECT 45 4D 47 30 30 30 30 30 30 30 answered 90 00.
The data file EF 3040 is thereOKThe first 16 bytes and the last byte (7FFF) both read, so the whole 32768-byte file is addressable.
The AID is in a registered categoryWARN45 4D 47 30 30 30 30 30 30 30 starts with 4. Registered AIDs start with A or D and proprietary ones with F. Apple has no written rule against it, but one developer saw iOS refuse such an AID, and only a real iPhone can settle it.

Session timeOK0.11 s used of the 60 s that iOS allows per session (an iPhone may be slower than this reader).

Android

YES: an Android app can find this card and use the applet.

OK: the whole card was read, 32768 bytes (32 KB, offsets 0000-7FFF).

What the Android does, step by step

StepResultDetails
ReaderOKIdentive CLOUD 4700 F Contactless Reader 0
Card type (ATR)OK3B 89 80 01 00 31 C1 73 C8 40 00 00 90 93 -> contactless smart card, ISO 14443-4 (ISO-DEP). A phone can send APDUs to this kind of card.
UID (Android: Tag.getId())INFO08 E6 11 9C (random UID: it changes at every tap, so an app must use GET UID 80 08 as the card ID)
Historical bytes (Android: IsoDep.getHistoricalBytes())INFO00 31 C1 73 C8 40 00 00 90
App AID listINFOnot needed on Android: IsoDep sends any APDU, and any app with the NFC permission can use it.
Tap: Android finds the cardOKAndroid checked for NDEF data (none), then gave the card to the app as IsoDep.
App sends: SELECT applet by AIDOK00 A4 04 00 0A 45 4D 47 30 30 30 30 30 30 30 -> 90 00 Success (32.8 ms)
App sends: GET UIDOK80 08 00 00 0A -> A1 FB 6A 0B F3 80 24 71 00 F7 90 00 Success (35.7 ms)
App sends: SELECT EF 3040OK00 A4 00 00 02 30 40 -> 90 00 Success (5.5 ms)
App sends: READ 256 bytes (Le 00)OK00 B0 00 00 00 -> 256 bytes 90 00 Success (29.6 ms)
App sends: SELECT another AIDINFO00 A4 04 00 05 A0 00 00 09 99 -> 6D 00 ORIGINAL applet answer (F5): this AID is not on the card, so the card OS passed the SELECT to the default applet; the ORIGINAL applet takes the AID as a file ID it does not know and answers 6D 00 (7.1 ms). Android lets the app select any AID.
Session timeINFO0.13 s. Android has no session limit; each command has a timeout (IsoDep.setTimeout).

29 checks: 28 of 29 passed, 0 failed. Extra checks: 14 of 14 passed. Android steps: 4 of 4 OK. Card size: 32768 bytes. Error map: 1 of the answers that did not pass are what the ORIGINAL applet sends (F5). Card data written back: yes.
